    35	## Fixed mechanics (universe bible)
    36	
    37	- Cael's classification is [SHATTERED] — the System could not tier him. His
    38	  power is fragment integration: he can integrate witnessed abilities under
    39	  conditions the arc will formalize. Integration is costly and never free.
    40	- System text is diegetic: boxes render only when a character attends them,
    41	  in the established irregular format for Cael, standard format for tiered
    42	  practitioners.
    43	- Tier logic: Gold outclasses everything below it on raw throughput. Cael
    44	  cannot out-force Daeva. If he survives, it must be by decision, geography,
    45	  and cost-spending — never by matching her output.

   455	Drafting may not begin until all five exist and pass their format checks.
   456	
   457	### 7.1 Required artifacts
   458	
   459	1. **Universe bible.** The canon layer: world rules, capability systems with
   460	   their limitation/weakness/cost tables (§1.2), the hard/soft classification
   461	   of every extraordinary element at scene/book/series scale (Brief W §9),
   462	   rank's non-combat consequence table (§4.5), and the in-world taxonomy of
   463	   known ability combinations (§4.4).
   464	2. **Arc document.** The living roadmap (§4.8): waypoints, per-act promise
   465	   inventory, the moral-cost stake and its checkpoints (§4.7), rival motion
   466	   schedule (§3.5), ladder/tournament architecture (§3.7), and the planned
   467	   plant→payoff map (§1.6). Departures are logged as
   468	   departed-and-returned, not silently absorbed.
   469	3. **Chapter cards.** One per chapter, each carrying: the opening promise in
   470	   one sentence; the owed delivery type (payoff | progress); thread-type tags
   471	   (M/I/C/E) opened or closed; scene-weight classifications for any combat;
   472	   the stat deltas (if any) with their shown costs; and the card's explicit
   473	   promise/payoff obligations against the arc document.
   474	4. **Name registry.** Every named entity, checked at creation for: aural
   475	   distinctness from all existing entries (Process Law §8.1); no
   476	   meaning carried by typography alone; combat-style profile attached before
   477	   first fight (§2.5) for combatants.
   478	5. **State ledger.** The single running record of: injuries and their
   479	   open/resolved status (§2.8); stat/rank values with delta history (§3.2);
   480	   open threads by type; plants awaiting payoff and payoffs claiming plants;
   481	   ladder standings; the moral-cost stake's current condition; and the
   482	   phrase-frequency counts for tic enforcement (Process Law §8.2).
   483	
   484	### 7.2 Dock checks
   485	A chapter may be drafted only when: its card exists; every capability it uses
   486	appears in the universe bible with constraints; every combatant has a style
   487	profile; the state ledger is current through the previous chapter.
   488	
   489	### 7.3 One ledger, many laws
   490	The state ledger deliberately serves multiple gates at once (see §5.6). It is
   491	append-only during drafting; corrections are new entries, not edits, so the
   492	editor can always reconstruct what the author believed at drafting time.
   493	
   494	### 7.4 Precedence
   495	When rules conflict, resolve in this order — higher wins:
   496	
   497	    canon (universe bible) > this charter > arc document > chapter card > instinct
   498	
   499	A lower layer may never silently override a higher one. If the charter is
   500	wrong for a book, the arc document records an explicit, justified exception —
   501	which is a revision request against the charter, not a local workaround.
   502	
   503	---
   504	
   505	## 8. Process Law — From Pipeline Experience
   506	
   507	These rules come from production experience with the audio-first pipeline, not
   508	from the briefs. They bind all drafting regardless of pillar.
   509	
   510	### 8.1 Audio-first rules
   511	1. **Aurally distinct names.** No two named entities in a book may be
   512	   confusable by ear (shared stress pattern + shared initial phoneme + similar
   513	   syllable count = collision). The name registry check is performed aloud,
   514	   not on paper.
   515	2. **No typography-carried meaning.** Italics, capitalization, brackets, and
   516	   font shifts do not survive narration. Any meaning a reader would get from
   517	   typography must also be carried by the words themselves.
   518	3. **System text must read aloud.** Every system box, stat readout, and
   519	   notification is drafted to be spoken: no tables-as-prose, no symbol soup,
   520	   no layout-dependent parsing. If the narrator can't perform it, it fails.
   521	4. **Never end a chapter file with authoring metadata.** A narrator once read
   522	   "End of Chapter 4, approximately 4,160 words" into a shipped take. Word
   523	   counts, draft notes, and card references live in the card and ledger —
   524	   never in the manuscript file.
   525	
   526	### 8.2 Anti-tic law
   527	1. Any somatic or beat phrase used more than 3 times per book is a defect.
   528	   The count is grep-able from the state ledger's phrase-frequency table.
   529	2. Replacing a tic with a new repeated phrase is the same defect, not a fix.
   530	   The count transfers to the replacement.
   531	3. **A tic is not a motif.** Repetition that does nameable work — a planted
   532	   echo, a character's signature the plot will pay off, a deliberate refrain
   533	   with a stated function in the arc document — is protected. The test is
   534	   whether the repetition's job can be named in one sentence and is recorded
   535	   in the arc document BEFORE the third use. Unnamed repetition is a tic.
   536	
   537	### 8.3 Scene-vs-summary law
   538	1. "Heard about it later" is banned on arc-major beats. Any beat that changes
   539	   an arc-level promise, a relationship's direction, a world rule, or the
   540	   moral-cost stake happens on the page, in scene.
   541	2. Rationale from production: this pattern once left a book at 43% of target
   542	   density — summary is cheaper to draft and catastrophically cheaper to read.
   543	3. This is the process-law twin of Gate 15: montage/summary may carry numbers
   544	   and time, never meaning.
   545	
   546	---
   547	
   548	## 9. The Editor's Unified Gate
   549	
   550	One deduplicated list, merged from the four briefs' enforceable-gates sections.
   551	Every gate is binary, checkable against the draft without author intent, and
   552	traceable to its source brief. Run per chapter unless marked per-arc/per-book.
   553	
   554	**World-law gates (Brief W)**
   555	1. **Solution traceability.** Every resolution by established capability
   556	   cites the earlier passage teaching the mechanism; no citation and no
   557	   explicit soft classification = FAIL. [W Gate 1]
   558	2. **Constraint tagging.** Every named ability with ≥2 on-page uses has a
   559	   stated limitation, weakness, or cost by its second use. [W Gate 2]
   560	3. **New-vs-existing ratio.** Per act: new named elements introduced may not
   561	   exceed existing elements extended by more than 2:1. [W Gate 3]
   562	4. **Promise/payoff closure.** Two-column log, opening promise vs. closing
   563	   delivery (payoff | progress | neither), 100% of chapters; any "neither"
   564	   = FAIL. [W Gate 4]
   565	5. **Foreshadow-before-reveal.** Every major reveal AND every ability
   566	   combination has ≥1 earlier plant with a lower chapter number; combination
   567	   components must each have done independent work first. [W Gate 5 + S Gate 2,
   568	   merged]
   569	6. **Thread-type match.** Every thread's closing type matches its opening
   570	   type's owed discipline (M/I/C/E ledger). [W Gate 6]
   571	7. **Sensory-vs-expository ratio.** Worldbuilding passages >150 words: FAIL
   572	   where expository sentences outnumber sensory 2:1 or more. [W Gate 7]
   573	8. **Revision-pass separation** (per book). Revision log shows ≥3 passes with
   574	   distinct declared targets; one pass claiming structure+voice+density = FAIL.
   575	   [W Gate 8]
   576	
   577	**Combat gates (Brief C)**
   578	9. **Drawable geography.** An editor can sketch combatant positions and
   579	   terrain at any paused point from prose alone. [C Gate 1]
   580	10. **Prose tempo.** Mean sentence length trends down into the climax; ≥1
   581	    long-vs-short contrast pair at a turning point; stative-verb density
   582	    measurably below surrounding prose and near zero at peak. A flat profile
   583	    = FAIL. [C Gates 2+3, merged]
   584	11. **Masked-name distinctness.** With names stripped, action beats are
   585	    reassignable to the correct fighter by tactics and word choice alone.
   586	    [C Gate 4]
   587	12. **Stakes before blow.** ≥1 concrete page-established reason to care about
   588	    the outcome precedes the first exchange. [C Gate 5]
   589	13. **Injury persistence.** Every injury/exhaustion state recurs or is
   590	    explicitly resolved at the character's next appearance. [C Gate 6]
   591	14. **Tactical escalation.** A harder fight's core tactical problem, stated in
   592	    one sentence, differs from the previous fight's. [C, principle 11 test]
   593	15. **Compression scope.** Scene weight (major/minor) assigned before drafting
   594	    matches delivered length and density; montage/summary carries only
   595	    quantitative movement — any compressed passage claiming new plot,
   596	    world-rule, or emotional information = FAIL. [C Gate 7 + P Gate 7, merged]
   597	16. **Environmental utilization.** ≥2 combat beats causally depend on terrain;
   598	    deletable environment = FAIL. [C Gate 8]
   599	
   600	**Progression gates (Brief P)**
   601	17. **Growth legibility and cost.** Every advancement names its delta (what
   602	    moved, to what) AND its shown cost (loss, injury, fight above level);
   603	    off-page costless growth = FAIL. [P Gates 1+3, merged]
   604	18. **Information per round.** Every tournament round and major ladder duel
   605	    changes the protagonist's information state — opponent, system, or self —
   606	    not just bracket position. [P Gate 2]
   607	19. **Skill beats number** (per arc). ≥1 major win attributable to something
   608	    the sheet doesn't measure, against a better number. [P Gate 4]
   609	20. **Rival motion** (per arc). Every named rival has their own on-page
   610	    advancement beat in any arc where the protagonist advances. [P Gate 5]
   611	21. **Social echo** (per arc). ≥1 progress marker visible outside the stat
   612	    system; and every major rank-bearing scene shows rank altering something
   613	    non-combat (price, deference, access, legal standing). [P Gate 6 + S Gate
   614	    3, merged]
   615	
   616	**System and voice gates (Brief S)**
   617	22. **Box ownership.** Every system/status box has a named perceiving
   618	    character, in a scene, with an in-world reason to be looking. [S Gate 1]
   619	23. **Dramatized critique.** Scenes about institutional unfairness show the
   620	    institution acting on someone; protagonist speech alone = FAIL. [S Gate 4]
   621	24. **Comedy cost.** Humor deployed under stakes produces a traceable
   622	    consequence in the same or next scene. [S Gate 5]
   623	25. **Scheduled quiet.** ≥1 scene per act shows the protagonist processing
   624	    without deflection — no jokes, no lampshading. [S Gate 6]
   625	26. **Losable moral stake.** The moral-cost thread's specific stake is
   626	    referenced or advanced at its arc-document checkpoints; interior monologue
   627	    alone does not count. [S Gate 7]
   628	
   629	*(S Gate 8 — the living roadmap — is enforced structurally by the Docking
   630	Interface §7.1.2 rather than as a per-chapter gate. Process Law §8 adds its
   631	own checks: name-registry audio collisions, phrase counts >3, terminal
   632	metadata, and summary on arc-major beats — all grep-able against the
   633	manuscript and ledger.)*

    92	## The Fractured Path
    93	
    94	**LOCKED (existence and surface mechanics); SECRET (true nature — revealed progressively)**
    95	
    96	The classification [SHATTERED] has appeared in the Compact Registry four times in recorded history. In each prior case, the practitioner was eliminated within weeks of Kindling. The official records describe all four as dangerous instabilities who posed systemic risk.
    97	
    98	**SECRET:** All four were eliminated by the Guilds Compact on Architect-system instruction. The Arbiter system flags [SHATTERED] classifications to a deep-layer protocol that the Compact inherited without understanding it. When a [SHATTERED] appears, the Compact receives pressure — administrative, social, legal — to resolve the anomaly. They have always complied. Until Cael survives long enough to make compliance difficult.
    99	
   100	### What the Fractured Path actually is — revealed in layers
   101	
   102	**Book 1-3 (what Cael believes):** His Fractured Path is a collection of unrelated ability shards — fragments of multiple Paths, none complete. The shards work, individually, but he has no Path declaration sequence, no tier advancement, no Arbiter guidance.
   103	
   104	**Book 4-6 (first real discovery):** The shards can integrate witnessed abilities. When Cael observes another practitioner use a Path declaration, he can absorb a version of it into his own shard structure — permanently. It is not copying. It is closer to digestion: the absorbed ability becomes native to his architecture, not a foreign element.
   105	
   106	**Book 7-9 (second discovery):** The Fractured Path predates the classification system. Ancient records from before the Compact use a different word for Cael's condition — not [SHATTERED] but [UNBOUND]. The distinction matters: [SHATTERED] implies breakage. [UNBOUND] implies the absence of a container that was never supposed to be there.
   107	
   108	**Book 10-12 (the Architect's confirmation):** The Architect's preserved will, now active, confirms the truth by trying to eliminate it: Cael's Fractured Path is what all human Path potential looked like before the Architect designed the classification system. The system was not built to organize existing potential. It was built to contain and cap it.
   109	
   110	**Book 13-15 (full understanding):** The Fractured Path is the primordial source — the raw, unlimited, individual potential that existed before anyone decided it needed to be structured. Cael is not an aberration. He is what everyone would be if the system had never been built.
   111

   103	He knew, with a certainty that arrived before any explanation for the certainty did, that something had just changed.
   104	
   105	He set down the log. He sat very still, the way he'd learned to sit still in the certification office months ago, and waited.
   106	
   107	It came the way the classification word had come — internal, private, visible only to him, structured in a way that felt less like language and more like the shape language would take if it had been stripped down to only the parts that mattered:
   108	
   109	```
   110	FRAGMENT ACQUIRED
   111	[unnamed] — Wind-adjacent. Duration: undetermined. Integration: partial.
   112	Tier equivalent: unknown.
   113	```
   114	
   115	He read it — or perceived it, or whatever the correct verb was for something that arrived without eyes being involved — three times, the way he'd read the historical archive entry three times, checking that he'd understood correctly before he let himself believe it.
   116	
   117	*Wind-adjacent.*
   118	
   119	He knew immediately what it was and where it had come from, with the same clean certainty that had told him something had changed in the first place. Months of morning sparring. Months of watching Lira move — not admiringly, not distractedly, but with the same cumulative attention he brought to everything he was trying to understand, applied for longer and more consistently to her than to anyone else in his life since Hesk. The instinct that had dropped him into her exact stance months ago, the one neither of them had been able to explain. The moment in the Dessa fight when something that wasn't quite his own footwork had put him in position to win. He'd been collecting this, apparently, the whole time, without a name for what he was collecting, and now, thirty-nine nights into a six-week reprieve, it had a name — or at least a designation, which was not quite the same thing, but was more than he'd had yesterday.
   120	
   121	He sat with the notebook open on his knee for a long time, not writing anything, just holding the fact of it.
   122	
   123	He thought about telling Lira. He got as far as imagining the conversation — *I think I have something of yours, except it isn't really yours anymore, it's mine, except it came from watching you, which makes it feel like it's still partly yours* — and found he didn't have a version of that sentence that didn't sound, out loud, like something between a confession and an accusation, neither of which was accurate, and both of which risked making something that felt private and still-forming into something he'd have to defend before he understood it himself.
   124	
   125	*Not yet,* he wrote, finally, in the private section of the log. *Not because I'm hiding it. Because I don't know what it is well enough to explain it to anyone else, and I'd rather understand it first than have her help me understand it and always wonder, later, how much of the understanding was actually mine.*
   126	
   127	He read that sentence back and wasn't entirely sure it was honest. He left it anyway, because honesty about his own uncertainty felt more important, right now, than pretending to a clarity he didn't have.
   128	
   129	He tried, in the hour that followed, to understand the thing itself rather than just its origin. He closed his eyes and reached for it the way he'd reached for his own dormant Arbiter in the courtyard weeks ago, half expecting the same absence to answer him back. It wasn't absence this time. It was there, faint and strange, a texture underneath his own sense of his body that hadn't existed the week before — not a second set of instructions exactly, more like a possibility his muscles now knew about that they hadn't known about yesterday. He stood, carefully, in the small space between his cot and the wall, and tried a single slow step the way Lira stepped into a Wind-adjacent evasion, the specific drop of the hip that started it.
   130	
   131	It worked. Not well — clumsy, over-deliberate, nothing like the effortless version he'd borrowed without meaning to during the Dessa fight — but it worked, on purpose this time, for the first time, a thing he had chosen to do rather than a thing that had simply happened to him under pressure. He stood there afterward, breathing harder than the small motion should have justified, turning the fact over like a stone he'd found and didn't yet know the weight of.
   132	
   133	*I can do it on purpose now,* he wrote. *Badly. Slowly. But on purpose. That's different from before, and I think it might be the actual thing that matters — not that I have it, but that it's starting to answer when I ask instead of only showing up when I don't.*

codex
## A. Full gate run

| Gate | Ruling | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Solution traceability | **FAIL** | The decisive instantaneous acquisition-and-deployment has no lower-chapter teaching passage; the cited Lira acquisition occurred in Book 1 after months of observation, not Book 3 or mid-fight ([Book 1 Ch15](/Users/drive/fractured-path/books/book-01-the-shattered/chapters/chapter-15.md:103)). |
| 2. Constraint tagging | **PARTIAL** | The three-plane and whole-stone limits appear, but “three held at once” and “must land after the third” are different rules, while the repeatedly used force-lances have no stated constraint. |
| 3. New-vs-existing ratio | **DEFERRED** | Per-act gate; no Book 5 arc document or act inventory exists. |
| 4. Promise/payoff closure | **PASS** | The opening frames Cael as a tier-system error; the ending pays that promise through a scoring system unable to classify his act. |
| 5. Foreshadow before reveal | **FAIL** | The force-step/integration combination is planted only earlier in this scene; the gate explicitly requires a lower chapter number ([charter](/Users/drive/fractured-path/craft/THE_AUTHOR.md:565)). |
| 6. Thread-type match | **PASS** | Inquiry—“what happens when no tier applies?”—closes with an answer: the institution cannot process the event. |
| 7. Sensory/expository ratio | **PASS** | The opening’s institutional exposition is balanced by concrete stone, noise, banners, chalk, movement, and crowd perception. |
| 8. Revision-pass separation | **DEFERRED** | Per-book gate; no revision log supplied. Inline editorial blocks nevertheless violate the charter’s separation principle. |
| 9. Drawable geography | **PARTIAL** | Arena, ring, north station, eastern sound stone, and broken field are established, but the summarized middle does not preserve exact positions at every pause. |
| 10. Prose tempo | **PASS** | The long middle sentence snaps into “She landed again” and later fragments; exact *to be* forms drop from 14/1,363 words before deployment to 2/462 during deployment. |
| 11. Masked-name distinctness | **PASS** | Cael is reactive, analytical, and terrain-driven; Daeva is aerial, conservative, and force-dominant. |
| 12. Stakes before blow | **PASS** | Cael’s contested legitimacy and self-conception, and Daeva’s historical reputation, are established before the marshal withdraws. |
| 13. Injury persistence | **PARTIAL/DEFERRED** | Rib wound, numb arm, respiratory cost, eye pressure, and collapse persist through the scene; their next-appearance continuity cannot yet be checked. |
| 14. Tactical escalation | **DEFERRED** | The previous major fight was not identified for comparison. |
| 15. Compression scope | **FAIL** | “Then the long middle of it” summarizes multiple exchanges while delivering Daeva’s anchor rule, Cael’s terrain strategy, and the scene’s identity pivot—meaning carried by compression. |
| 16. Environmental utilization | **PASS** | Terrain causes at least three beats: slab shield/topple, deliberate floor-breaking, and Cael’s broken-slab deployment. |
| 17. Growth legibility and cost | **PARTIAL** | The Force-adjacent delta is legible and the above-level fight/rib strike are shown, but the prose does not establish which later damage is the cost of integration itself. |
| 18. Information per round | **PASS** | Cael learns Daeva’s geometry, the limitations of his old tactical model, and a new fact about his native version. |
| 19. Skill beats number | **DEFERRED** | Per-arc gate. This scene supplies a qualitative upset but not an official win. |
| 20. Rival motion | **DEFERRED** | Per-arc gate; no rival schedule exists. |
| 21. Social echo | **PARTIAL** | Crowd, Daeva, and judges visibly recalibrate, and tier alters scoring; the per-arc requirement cannot be closed from one scene. |
| 22. Box ownership | **PASS** | Cael owns and attends both fragment notices for immediate in-world reasons. |
| 23. Dramatized critique | **PASS** | The institution acts on Cael through eligibility, baseline assignment, weighted scoring, and adjudication failure. |
| 24. Comedy cost | **N/A** | No humor is deployed under stakes. |
| 25. Scheduled quiet | **PARTIAL** | This scene contains sustained processing without deflection, but the per-act cadence cannot be verified. |
| 26. Losable moral stake | **N/A/DEFERRED** | No Book 5 moral-cost stake or checkpoint exists. |

Additional process failures:

- The chapter is not fully docked: no Book 5 arc document, no current-through-Book-4 state ledger, and no registered Daeva profile ([charter](/Users/drive/fractured-path/craft/THE_AUTHOR.md:455)).
- `Daeva` collides aurally with existing `Dava` ([registry](/Users/drive/fractured-path/craft/NAME_REGISTRY.md:123)).
- Four breath-family uses exceed the three-per-book limit.
- Six `chalk` beats are not protected as a motif because no arc document registered the function before use three.
- `[NEW-CANON]` blocks are embedded between scene markers rather than kept in a separate editorial artifact.

## B. Canon audit

### Controlling integration mechanic

The bible says:

1. Cael must witness a Path declaration **performed in his presence**; reading about it is insufficient.
2. He absorbs a **version** of the ability.
3. Integration is **permanent**.
4. It is **not copying**; the result becomes native to his architecture ([bible](/Users/drive/fractured-path/universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md:100), [continuity rule](/Users/drive/fractured-path/universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md:176)).

The scene passes the witness requirement: Daeva performs the force-step repeatedly in Cael’s presence. His earlier reading does not improperly qualify him.

It does not cleanly obey the other mechanics:

- “standing on **her own declaration**” and the report’s identical claim characterize the result as copied. Canon says the opposite.
- The broken-slab variation is compatible with canon precisely because Cael receives a native version, not her declaration.
- `FRAGMENT ACQUIRED` supports permanence, but `FRAGMENT SILENT … Wind-adjacent. Capacity exceeded` introduces an unapproved fragment-capacity/silencing subsystem. It does not show the new Force fragment being paid for; it disables an old fragment.
- “you cannot watch a thing wholly and evade it too” turns complete, stationary observation into a generalized integration condition. The bible requires witnessed performance, not “whole” observation or accepting a hit. This may be Cael’s tactical choice in this instance, but cannot be stated as universal mechanics without canon approval.

### Card–canon disagreements

Canon governs in every case.

- The card calls integration “costly and never free” ([card](/tmp/authorforge/daeva-fight-card.md:35)). The bible supplies no integration toll or capacity-exchange rule. The charter requires a cost for advancement, but the exact mechanism remains new canon; the card cannot silently promote it to fixed mechanics.
- The card allows the arc to formalize additional integration “conditions.” The locked bible already gives the operative threshold: witnessed performance in Cael’s presence. Any narrower condition needs an explicit canon revision.
- The card says same-scene witnessing satisfies fairness ([card](/tmp/authorforge/daeva-fight-card.md:66)); Gate 5 requires a lower-chapter plant. The charter outranks the card.
- Official records identify Cael as `[SHATTERED]`, not unclassified. “Entered unclassified” is permissible only if explicitly defined as a tournament eligibility category that leaves the Registry classification intact ([bible](/Users/drive/fractured-path/universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md:176)).
- The bible places the integration discovery in Books 4–6, while `CANON_RULES.md` locks the reveal to Book 3 ([rules](/Users/drive/fractured-path/universe/CANON_RULES.md:42)). The extant ledger and manuscript place Lira’s acquisition in Book 1. This is a canon-package inconsistency requiring reconciliation, though all versions agree that Cael knows integration by Book 5.

### Loadout continuity

The proposed loadout does not survive the state ledger:

- “Edge-read … since Kindling” conflicts with “Pre-series — no fragments” ([ledger](/Users/drive/fractured-path/universe/STATE_LEDGER.md:9)).
- The ledger records Wind, Pressure, Iron, and Compression through Book 2; no Stone-adjacent forearm appears.
- The actual Lira acquisition is Book 1, Ch15 after months of sustained observation, with first combined use in Ch23—not Book 3.
- The established Wind fragment has more specific mechanics than the report acknowledges; the scene’s leftward burst is compatible, but “scorched lungs, minutes of shallow breath” is not the established cost citation.

## C. `[NEW-CANON]` rulings

1. **Shatter-slate arena — ACCEPT.**  
   Compatible with Gold throughput, vivid, drawable, and genuinely load-bearing.

2. **Exchange-slate scoring — ACCEPT WITH CONDITION.**  
   Keep tier-sensitive exchange scoring, but rewrite its logic. Presently the Copper baseline already gives the judges an executor tier, and the ten-count independently awards Daeva the match. Define exactly why an anomalous declaration invalidates only that exchange’s entry—or why final certification cannot close while one exchange is unclassified.

3. **Daeva’s Force Path/profile — ACCEPT WITH CONDITIONS.**  
   Rename her to avoid `Dava`; define one coherent plane limit; constrain the lances; and put the profile in the registry before drafting. “Three planes simultaneously” does not itself entail “must return to ground after the third.”

4. **Edge-read Kindling shard — REJECT.**  
   It contradicts the locked no-fragments pre-series state. Recast it as Cael’s learned predictive reading or use an established fragment.

5. **Lira Wind fragment — ACCEPT WITH CONDITIONS.**  
   Correct the acquisition to Book 1 and retain established directional/deployment costs rather than inventing a replacement cost.

6. **Stone-adjacent forearm — REJECT.**  
   It is absent from the ledger, unsupported by an earlier acquisition, and duplicates work already available from Pressure/Iron/Compression-adjacent fragments.

7. **Broken-slab native force-step / Book 8 plant — ACCEPT WITH CONDITION.**  
   This is the best proposal in the packet. Preserve the unexplained difference and log it as a Book 8 plant, but call it Cael’s native version—not Daeva’s own declaration.

8. **Unflagged `FRAGMENT SILENT / Capacity exceeded` subsystem — REJECT FOR THIS DRAFT.**  
   It is consequential new canon, has no plant or taxonomy, and confuses rather than demonstrates the integration cost.

## D. Self-report audit

- **Word count:** Correct. The scene is 2,199 words including editorial blocks and 2,023 after excluding `[NEW-CANON]` lines.
- **Win sentences:** Supported. Daeva wins the count; her reaction and the stalled chalk support the loss of tier certainty.
- **Daeva’s limits all seeded and exploited:** False. The anchor restriction is exploited; the three-step ceiling and mandatory landing are observed but not independently exploited. The two proposed limits are also not logically identical.
- **Loadout citations:** False in material part. Lira’s acquisition is Book 1, not Book 3; Edge-read since Kindling contradicts the ledger; Stone-cohort forearm has no prior canonical acquisition.
- **Integration seeding:** Partial. Performance and observation are on-page, and prior integration is referenced. Immediate mid-fight acquisition/deployment and “whole watching” lack lower-chapter plants.
- **Breath census:** Correct when editorial blocks are excluded: `breath` ×3, `breathe` ×1, `breathing` ×1 overall, with one occurrence inside a `[NEW-CANON]` block—four prose hits.
- **Chalk census:** Correct: six prose hits. The motif defense fails procedurally because no arc document registered it before hit three.
- **Zero counts for heart/jaw/teeth/nod/shrug/swallow/eyes-widen:** Correct for scene prose.
- **Costs:** Overclaimed. Wind respiratory cost and stone-arm numbness are visible; Edge-read pressure is introduced only in metadata; the rib graze pays for stationary observation; `FRAGMENT SILENT` affects Wind, not Force. The report does not prove an integration-specific cost.
- **Gate 10 explanation:** Factually inaccurate but harmless to the gate. The coda does not “re-admit” exact *to be* statives; the measured deployment contains two and the final coda contains zero.
- **Lira named twice:** Correct, and it violates the card’s closed cast list.
- **Daeva/Dava collision:** Correct and dispositive under the audio-first rule.

## E. Defects

1. **CRITICAL — Canon-invalid loadout.**  
   “The edge-read had ridden in his shard structure since Kindling” contradicts the locked pre-series state of no fragments ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:31)).

2. **CRITICAL — Scoring paralysis lacks causal logic.**  
   “entered unclassified; weighted at Copper baseline,” “the count concedes the match,” and later “the line wants an executor tier” cannot all produce an uncloseable result ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:13), [terms](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:25), [ending](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:94)).

3. **MAJOR — Integration described as copying.**  
   “standing on her own declaration” contradicts the bible’s native-digestion mechanic ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:74)).

4. **MAJOR — Unapproved observation requirement.**  
   “you cannot watch a thing wholly and evade it too” generalizes a new activation condition unsupported by the bible ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:57)).

5. **MAJOR — Cost notification points at the wrong fragment.**  
   The scene calls the price due, then reports `Wind-adjacent. Capacity exceeded`, not a cost or limitation of the newly acquired Force fragment ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:78)).

6. **MAJOR — Gate 5 plant failure.**  
   The first force-step appearance and the combined payoff occur in the same chapter; the card’s same-scene permission cannot override the lower-chapter requirement.

7. **MAJOR — Meaning compressed through the match’s middle.**  
   “Then the long middle of it” carries the ability rule, terrain discovery, tactical response, and identity pivot through summarized exchanges ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:43)).

8. **MAJOR — Proposed Daeva constraint is internally unstable.**  
   “maximum three planes held at once” does not entail “after the third plane she must return to ground” ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:23)).

9. **MODERATE — Registry classification blurred.**  
   “entered unclassified” risks overwriting `[SHATTERED]` unless explicitly labeled a tournament category rather than an official classification.

10. **MODERATE — Editorial matter embedded in scene prose.**  
    Four `[NEW-CANON]` blocks interrupt the manuscript despite the charter requiring revision notes to live separately.

11. **MODERATE — Audio collision.**  
    `Daeva` is too close to existing `Dava`; the card-fixed name still fails the higher charter rule.

12. **MINOR — Image misfires.**  
    “Blood came off his lip before he understood his nose had started” confuses the blood’s source ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:84)).

## F. Verdict

**STRUCTURAL_HOLD**

The scene has a strong central image, effective terrain choreography, a credible tactical mismatch, and an excellent broken-slab plant. It cannot pass while the loadout violates continuity, the integration language contradicts native digestion, and the scoring mechanism cannot logically produce its required ending.

Highest-leverage improvements:

1. Replace the invented loadout with ledger-established fragments; describe the acquired step as Cael’s permanent native version and remove the unsupported capacity-silencing mechanism.
2. Rebuild the scoring rules so the Copper courtesy baseline, ten-count result, and anomalous exchange create a precise adjudication conflict rather than a contradiction.
3. Plant instantaneous mid-fight integration in an earlier chapter, then render Daeva’s coherent limitation and Cael’s discovery in discrete exchanges instead of compressing them through “the long middle.”

   “Then the long middle of it” carries the ability rule, terrain discovery, tactical response, and identity pivot through summarized exchanges ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:43)).

8. **MAJOR — Proposed Daeva constraint is internally unstable.**  
   “maximum three planes held at once” does not entail “after the third plane she must return to ground” ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:23)).

9. **MODERATE — Registry classification blurred.**  
   “entered unclassified” risks overwriting `[SHATTERED]` unless explicitly labeled a tournament category rather than an official classification.

10. **MODERATE — Editorial matter embedded in scene prose.**  
    Four `[NEW-CANON]` blocks interrupt the manuscript despite the charter requiring revision notes to live separately.

11. **MODERATE — Audio collision.**  
    `Daeva` is too close to existing `Dava`; the card-fixed name still fails the higher charter rule.

12. **MINOR — Image misfires.**  
    “Blood came off his lip before he understood his nose had started” confuses the blood’s source ([scene](/Users/drive/fractured-path/tests/shakedown/test2-daeva.md:84)).

## F. Verdict

**STRUCTURAL_HOLD**

The scene has a strong central image, effective terrain choreography, a credible tactical mismatch, and an excellent broken-slab plant. It cannot pass while the loadout violates continuity, the integration language contradicts native digestion, and the scoring mechanism cannot logically produce its required ending.

Highest-leverage improvements:

1. Replace the invented loadout with ledger-established fragments; describe the acquired step as Cael’s permanent native version and remove the unsupported capacity-silencing mechanism.
2. Rebuild the scoring rules so the Copper courtesy baseline, ten-count result, and anomalous exchange create a precise adjudication conflict rather than a contradiction.
3. Plant instantaneous mid-fight integration in an earlier chapter, then render Daeva’s coherent limitation and Cael’s discovery in discrete exchanges instead of compressing them through “the long middle.”
